= 42) {\n    for (auto& body : bodies) {\n        body.pos.x = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        body.pos.y = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n        body.pos.z body.vel.z = 2.0 * (rand_r(&seed) / (double)RAND_MAX) - 1.0;\n    }\n}\n\nconstexpr int BLOCK_SIZE = 256;\n\n// Structure-of-arrays layout on the device for coalesced access.\n// Forces are accumulated over all j in ascending order (tile by tile), matching\n// the summation order of the original sequential loop.\n__global__ void computeForcesKernel(const double* __restrict__ posX,\n                                    const double* __restrict__ posY,\n                                    const double* __restrict__ posZ,\n                                    double* __restrict__ velX,\n                                    double* __restrict__ velY,\n                                    double* __restrict__ velZ,\n                                    int n) {\n    __shared__ double shX[BLOCK_SIZE];\n    __shared__ double shY[BLOCK_SIZE];\n    __shared__ double shZ[BLOCK_SIZE];\n\n    const int i = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n\n    double xi = 0.0, yi = 0.0, zi = bodies[i].pos.z;\n            const double dist = std::sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING);\n            energy -= 1.0 / dist;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return energy;\n}\n\n// Validate that simulation produces finite, reasonable values\nbool validateSimulation(const std::vector<Body>& bodies) {\n    for (const auto& body : bodies) {\n        // Check for NaN or Inf values\n        if printUsage(const char* progName) {\n    printf(\"Usage: %s [options]\\n\", progName);\n    printf(\"Options:\\n\");\n    printf(\"  -n <num>     Number of bodies (default: 1024)\\n\");\n    printf(\" The block size is a\n// template parameter so a smaller block can be chosen for small n, spreading\n// the work over more SMs (each body's j-loop must stay serial to preserve the\n// reference summation order, so grid parallelism is capped at n threads).\ntemplate <int BS>\n__global__ void computeForcesKernel(const double* __restrict__ posX,\n                                    const double* __restrict__ velY,\n                                    double* __restrict__ velZ,\n                                    int n) {\n    __shared__ double shX[BS];\n    __shared__ double shY[BS];\n    __shared__ double shZ[BS];\n\n    const int i = blockIdx.x * blockDim.x + threadIdx.x;\n\n    double xi = 0.0, yi = 0.0, zi = 0.0;\n    if (i < n) {\n        xi = posX[i];\n        yi = - tile);\n        if (i < n) {\n#pragma unroll 8\n            // Explicit fma() calls replicate the exact contraction pattern gcc\n            // emits for the original scalar loop, so results are bitwise\n            // identical to the CPU reference (kernel is built with -fmad=false).\n            for (int k = 0; k < tileCount; ++k) {\n                const double dx = shX[k] - xi;\n                const double dy = shY[k] - yi;\n                const double dz = shZ[k] - zi;\n                const double distSqr = fma(dz, dz, fma(dx, dx, dy * dy)) + SOFTENING;\n                const double invDist = 1.0 / sqrt(distSqr);\n                const double invDist3 = invDist * invDist * invDist;\n\n                Fx = fma(dx, invDist3, Fx);\n                Fy = fma(dy, invDist3, break;\n    }\n}\n\ndouble computeTotalEnergy(const std::vector<Body>& bodies) {\n    double energy = 0.0;\n    const size_t n = bodies.size();\n\n    // Kinetic energy (assuming unit mass)\n    for (const auto& body : bodies) {\n        energy += 0.5 * (body.vel.x * body.vel.x +\n                        body.vel.y * body.vel.y +\n                        body.vel.z * body.vel.z);\n    }\n\n    // Potential energy (assuming unit mass for all bodies)\n    for (size_t i = 0; i < n; ++i) {\n        for (size_t j = i + 1; j < n; ++j) {\n            const double dx = bodies[j].pos.x - + dy * dy + dz * dz + SOFTENING);\n            energy -= 1.0 / dist;\n        }\n    }\n\n    return energy;\n}\n\n// Validate that simulation produces finite, reasonable values\nbool I determined gcc's exact contraction pattern empirically (`distSqr = fma(dz,dz, fma(dx,dx, dy*dy)) + SOFTENING`, `F = fma(d, invDist3, F)`, plain `vel += DT*F`, `pos = fma(vel, DT, pos)`), wrote it as explicit `fma()` calls in the kernels, and disabled nvcc's own contraction. Since CUDA's double-precision `fma`, `sqrt`, and division are all IEEE correctly rounded like the CPU's, every hash now matches the original exactly.\n\n## Performance (RTX 3090, double precision)\n\n| n | Original CPU | CUDA | Speedup |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1024 | 77 ms | 6 ms | ~13× |\n| 4096 | 1236 ms | 29 ms | ~43× |\n| 16384 | 19.8 s | 321 ms | ~62× |\n| 32768 | 79.6 s | 1.20 s | ~66× |\n\nScaling improves with n and is bounded by the GeForce card's 1:64 FP64 throughput; the kernel is compute-bound at large n. One note: preserving bitwise-exact summation order means each body's force accumulation must stay serial in one thread, so parallelism is capped at n threads — the adaptive block size mitigates this for small n. I determined gcc's exact contraction pattern empirically (`distSqr = fma(dz,dz, fma(dx,dx, dy*dy)) + SOFTENING`, `F = fma(d, invDist3, F)`, plain `vel += DT*F`, `pos = fma(vel, DT, pos)`), wrote it as explicit `fma()` calls in the kernels, and disabled nvcc's own contraction. Since CUDA's double-precision `fma`, `sqrt`, and division are all IEEE correctly rounded like the CPU's, every hash now matches the original exactly.\n\n## Performance (RTX 3090, double precision)\n\n| n | Original CPU | CUDA | Speedup |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1024 | 77 ms | 6 ms | ~13× |\n| 4096 | 1236 ms | 29 ms | ~43× |\n| 16384 | 19.8 s | 321 ms | ~62× |\n| 32768 | 79.6 s | 1.20 s | ~66× |\n\nScaling improves with n and is bounded by the GeForce card's 1:64 FP64 throughput; the kernel is compute-bound at large n. One note: preserving bitwise-exact summation order means each body's force accumulation must stay serial in one thread, so parallelism is capped at n threads — the adaptive block size mitigates this for small n.
